374→\n   375→\treturn backoff.RetryNotify(operation, backoffCfg, notify)\n   376→}\n   377→\n   378→// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   379→// Drift Metric Implementations\n   380→// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   381→\n   382→// populationStabilityIndex computes PSI using baseline and observed buckets.\n   383→// Formula: PSI = Σ ((obs_i – base_i) * ln(obs_i / base_i))\n   384→func populationStabilityIndex(baseline, observed []int64) (float64, error) {\n   385→\tif len(baseline) != float64(baseline[i]) / float64(totalBase)\n   397→\t\tobsRatio := float64(observed[i]) / float64(totalObs)\n   398→\n   399→\t\t// apply Laplace smoothing to avoid zero ratios\n   400→\t\tif baseRatio == 0 {\n   401→\t\t\tbaseRatio = 0.0001\n   402→\t\t}\n   403→\t\tif obsRatio == 0 {\n   404→\t\t\tobsRatio = 0.0001\n   405→\t\t}\n   406→\t\tpsi += (obsRatio - baseRatio) * math.Log(obsRatio/baseRatio)\n   407→\t}\n   408→\treturn psi, nil\n   409→}\n   410→\n   411→func sumInt64(xs []int64) int64 {\n   412→\tvar s int64\n   413→\tfor _, v := range xs {\n   414→\t\ts += v\n   415→\t}\n   416→\treturn s\n   417→}\n   418→\n   419→// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   420→// Helper: Simple bootstrap when executed as a skip or request baseline?\n\t\tm.logger.Warn(\"no baseline found; skipping\",\n\t\t\tzap.String(\"model\", ev.ModelID),\n\t\t\tzap.String(\"feature\", ev.FeatureName))\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tbaseline := baseRaw.([]int64)\n\n\tpsi, err := m.driftMetric(baseline, ev.BucketCounts)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tm.psiGauge.WithLabelValues(ev.ModelID, ev.FeatureName).Set(psi)\n\tm.evProcessed.Add(1)\n\n\tif psi >= m.cfg.Threshold {\n\t\tm.evDrifted.Add(1)\n\t\tm.driftCounter.WithLabelValues(ev.ModelID, ev.FeatureName).Inc()\n\n\t\tdrift := DriftEvent{\n\t\t\tModelID:     ev.ModelID,\n\t\t\tFeatureName: ev.FeatureName,\n\t\t\tMetric:      \"PSI\",\n\t\t\tScore:       psi,\n\t\t\tThreshold:   m.cfg.Threshold,\n\t\t\tTimestamp:   time.Now().UnixMilli(),\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn m.publishDriftEvent(ctx, drift)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) publishDriftEvent(ctx context.Context, ev DriftEvent) error {\n\tpayload, err := json.Marshal(ev)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tmsg := &sarama.ProducerMessage{\n\t\tTopic: m.cfg.OutputTopic,\n\t\tValue: sarama.ByteEncoder(payload),\n\t\tKey:   sarama.StringEncoder(ev.ModelID + \"|\" + ev.FeatureName),\n\t}\n\n\toperation := func() error {\n\t\t_, _, err := m.producer.SendMessage(msg)\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tnotify := func(err error, t time.Duration) {\n\t\tm.logger.Warn(\"failed to publish drift event; retrying\",\n\t\t\tzap.Error(err), zap.Duration(\"next_retry_in\", t))\n\t}\n\n\tbackoffCfg := backoff.NewExponentialBackOff()\n\tbackoffCfg.MaxElapsedTime = 30 * time.Second\n\n\treturn backoff.RetryNotify(operation, backoffCfg, notify)\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Drift Metric Implementations\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// populationStabilityIndex computes PSI using baseline and observed buckets.\n// Formula: PSI = Σ ((obs_i – base_i) * ln(obs_i / base_i))\nfunc populationStabilityIndex(baseline, observed []int64) (float64, error) {\n\tif len(baseline) != len(observed) {\n\t\treturn 0, errors.New(\"bucket length mismatch\")\n\t}\n\ttotalBase := sumInt64(baseline)\n\ttotalObs := sumInt64(observed)\n\tif totalBase == 0 || totalObs == 0 {\n\t\treturn 0, errors.New(\"empty distributions\")\n\t}\n\n\tvar psi float64\n\tfor i := range baseline {\n\t\tbaseRatio := float64(baseline[i]) / float64(totalBase)\n\t\tobsRatio := float64(observed[i]) / float64(totalObs)\n\n\t\t// apply Laplace smoothing to avoid zero ratios\n\t\tif baseRatio == 0 {\n\t\t\tbaseRatio = 0.0001\n\t\t}\n\t\tif obsRatio == 0 {\n\t\t\tobsRatio = 0.0001\n\t\t}\n\t\tpsi += (obsRatio - baseRatio) * math.Log(obsRatio/baseRatio)\n\t}\n\treturn psi, nil\n}\n\nfunc sumInt64(xs []int64) int64 {\n\tvar s int64\n\tfor _, v := range xs {\n\t\ts += v\n\t}\n\treturn s\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Helper: Simple bootstrap when executed as a standalone binary.\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// When this file is built as `drift-monitor` cmd, we allow running directly\n// (useful in local docker-compose). 247→\t\t\td.cfg.Logger.Errorf(\"handleMessage error: %v\", err)\n   248→\t\t}\n   249→\t\td.metrics.processingDuration.Observe(time.Since(start).Seconds())\n   250→\t}\n   251→}\n   252→\n   253→// handleMessage parses the Kafka message and decides whether to trigger retrain.\n   254→func (d *DriftMonitor) handleMessage(m kafka.Message) error {\n   255→\tvar payload MetricsPayload\n   256→\tif features.\n   261→\tvar psiSum float64\n   262→\tvar count int\n   263→\tfor _, bucket := range payload.FeatureDrifts {\n   264→\t\tpsiSum += populationStabilityIndex(bucket.Expected, bucket.Observed)\n   265→\t\tcount++\n   266→\t}\n   267→\tif count == 0 {\n   268→\t\treturn errors.New(\"empty feature drift data\")\n   269→\t}\n   270→\tavgPSI := psiSum / float64(count)\n   271→\td.metrics.driftGauge.WithLabelValues(payload.ModelID).Set(avgPSI)\n   272→\n   273→\tif d.cfg.Logger != nil {\n   274→\t\td.cfg.Logger.Debugf(\"model=%s version=%s avgPSI=%.4f\", payload.ModelID, payload.ModelVersion, avgPSI)\n   275→\t}\n   276→\n   277→\t// Determine if retrain is needed.\n   278→\tif avgPSI >= d.cfg.DriftThreshold {\n   279→\t\treq := RetrainRequest{\n   280→\t\t\tModelID:        payload.ModelID,\n   281→\t\t\tCurrentVersion: payload.ModelVersion,\n   282→\t\t\tDriftMagnitude: avgPSI,\n   283→\t\t\tTriggeredAt:    time.Now().UnixMilli(),\n   284→\t\t\tReason:         fmt.Sprintf(\"PSI above threshold (%.2f >= %.2f)\", avgPSI, d.cfg.DriftThreshold),\n   285→\t\t\tCorrelationID:  fmt.Sprintf(\"%s_%d\", payload.ModelID, m.Offset),\n   286→\t\t}\n   287→\t\tb, _ := json.Marshal(&req)\n   288→\t\tmsg := kafka.Message{\n   289→\t\t\tKey:   []byte(req.ModelID),\n   290→\t\t\tValue: b,\n   291→\t\t}\n   292→\t\tif err := d.writer.WriteMessages(d.ctx, msg); err != nil {\n   293→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"write retrain request: %w\", err)\n   294→\t\t}\n   295→\t\td.metrics.retrainCounter.WithLabelValues(req.ModelID).Inc()\n   296→\t\tif d.cfg.Logger != nil {\n   297→\t\t\td.cfg.Logger.Infof(\"Triggered retrain for %s (PSI=%.3f)\", req.ModelID, avgPSI)\n   298→\t\t}\n   299→\t}\n   300→\treturn nil\n   301→}\n   302→\n   303→// populationStabilityIndex computes PSI between expected and observed histograms.\n   304→// It returns math.NaN() if arrays have different sizes or contain invalid values.\n   305→//\n   306→// Note: In production, you might move this to its own math/stats package and\n   307→// use higher precision numerics.  Here we apply a pragmatic approach.\n   308→func populationStabilityIndex(expected, observed []float64) float64 {\n   309→\tif len(expected) != len(observed) || len(expected) == 0 {\n   310→\t\treturn math.NaN()\n   311→\t}\n   312→\n   313→\tvar psi float64\n   314→\tfor i, e := range expected {\n   315→\t\to := observed[i]\n   316→\t\tif e <= 0 || o <= 0 {\n   317→\t\t\tcontinue // skip zero buckets to avoid divide-by-zero/log\n   318→\t\t}\n   319→\t\tpsi += (o - e) * math.Log(o/e)\n   320→\t}\n   321→\treturn psi\n   322→}\n   323→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Compute aggregate drift across all features.\n\tvar psiSum float64\n\tvar count int\n\tfor _, bucket := range payload.FeatureDrifts {\n\t\tpsiSum += values.\n//\n// Note: In production, you might move this to its own math/stats package and\n// use higher precision numerics.  Here we apply a pragmatic approach.\nfunc populationStabilityIndex(expected, observed []float64) float64 {\n\tif len(expected) != len(observed) || len(expected) == 0 {\n\t\treturn math.NaN()\n\t}\n\n\tvar psi float64\n\tfor i, e := range expected {\n\t\to := observed[i]\n\t\tif e <= 0 || o <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue // skip zero buckets to avoid divide-by-zero/log\n\t\t}\n\t\tpsi += (o - e) * math.Log(o/e)\n\t}\n\treturn psi\n}\n```","numLines":323,"startLine":1,"totalLines":323}}}

The code can be dropped into a larger\n// code-base or run standalone for demonstration purposes.\n//\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------\n\npackage monitor // import \"github.com/echopulse/ep/monitor\"\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"math\"\n\t\"sort\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\tkafka \"github.com/segmentio/kafka-go\"\n\t\"gonum.org/v1/gonum/stat\"\n)\n\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Public Domain Types\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// FeatureVector represents a canonicalised, numeric feature vector\n// generated by an upstream pipeline stage. `json:\"window_size\"`\n\tBaselineSize int       `json:\"baseline_size\"`\n\tModelVer     string    `json:\"model_version\"`\n}\n\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Strategy Pattern → Statistical Test Interface\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// StatisticalTest abstracts a two-sample test that returns the\n// p-value for the null-hypothesis \"samples originate from the same\n// distribution\".\ntype StatisticalTest interface {\n\tPValue(sampleA, sampleB []float64) (float64, error)\n\tName() string\n}\n\n// registry maps symbolic names onto factory functions so that tests\n// can be configured in TOML/YAML/etc. at runtime.\nvar registry = map[string]func() StatisticalTest{\n\t\"ks\":     func() StatisticalTest { return &KSTest{} },\n\t\"mannwh\": func() StatisticalTest { return &MannWhitneyUTest{} },\n}\n\n// GetTest returns an initialised StatisticalTest by name.\nfunc GetTest(name string) (StatisticalTest, error) {\n\tf, ok := registry[name]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unknown statistical test %q\", name)\n\t}\n\treturn f(), nil\n}\n\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Concrete Strategies\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// KSTest – two-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov (continuous features).\ntype KSTest struct{}\n\nfunc (KSTest) Name() string { return \"ks\" }\n\nfunc (KSTest) PValue(a, b []float64) (float64, error) {\n\tif len(a) < 2 || len(b) < 2 {\n\t\treturn 1.0, errors.New(\"insufficient sample size for ks test\")\n\t}\n\tstatistic := ksStatistic(a, b)\n\tp := ksAsymptoticP(statistic, len(a), len(b))\n\treturn p, nil\n}\n\n// Mann-Whitney-U (a.k.a. Wilcoxon Rank-Sum) – robust to outliers.\ntype MannWhitneyUTest struct{}\n\nfunc (MannWhitneyUTest) Name() string { return \"mannwh\" }\n\nfunc (MannWhitneyUTest) PValue(a, b []float64) (float64, error) {\n\tif len(a) < 2 || len(b) < 2 {\n\t\treturn 1.0, errors.New(\"insufficient sample size for mann-whitney test\")\n\t}\n\tp := stat.MannWhitneyUTest(a, b, nil)\n\treturn p, nil\n}\n\n// ksStatistic computes the two-sample KS-D statistic.\nfunc ksStatistic(x, y []float64) float64 {\n\txs := append([]float64(nil), x...)\n\tys := append([]float64(nil), y...)\n\tsort.Float64s(xs)\n\tsort.Float64s(ys)\n\n\tnx, ny := len(xs), len(ys)\n\ti, j := 0, 0\n\tvar dMax float64\n\tfor i < nx && j < ny {\n\t\tvx, vy := xs[i], ys[j]\n\t\tswitch {\n\t\tcase vx <= vy:\n\t\t\ti++\n\t\tcase vy < vx:\n\t\t\tj++\n\t\t}\n\t\tcdfX := float64(i) / float64(nx)\n\t\tcdfY := float64(j) / float64(ny)\n\t\tdiff := math.Abs(cdfX - cdfY)\n\t\tif diff > dMax {\n\t\t\tdMax = diff\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn dMax\n}\n\n// ksAsymptoticP calculates the asymptotic KS p-value.\nfunc ksAsymptoticP(d float64, n, m int) float64 {\n\tne := float64(n*m) / float64(n+m)\n\tlambda := (math.Sqrt(ne) + 0.12 + 0.11/math.Sqrt(ne)) * d\n\t// 2 * Σ (-1)^{k-1} exp(-2 k² λ²)  (k from 1..∞).  Truncate at 5 terms.\n\tvar sum float64\n\tfor k := 1; k <= 5; k++ {\n\t\tterm := math.Exp(-2 * float64(k*k) * lambda * lambda)\n\t\tif k%2 == 1 {\n\t\t\tsum += term\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tsum -= term\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn math.Max(math.Min(2*sum, 1.0), 0.0)\n}\n\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// EventBus Abstraction + Kafka Publisher Implementation\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// EventBus is deliberately minimal. A TrendSurfer\n// subscribes to the SocialEvent bus, maintains a sliding time-window of token\n// frequencies, and emits “trend” events whenever the set of top-K terms drifts\n// significantly.\n//\n// The component is self-contained and exposes a small API so that callers\n// (gRPC handlers, CLI tools, other pipeline stages) can:\n//\n//   • Start / stop the surfer with context cancelation\n//   • Query the current top-K trends in constant time\n//   • Register a callback that fires whenever a fresh trend vector is emitted\n//\n// Internally the TrendSurfer:\n//\n//   1. Start() must be\n// called to begin processing.\nfunc New(cfg Config) (*DriftMonitor, error) {\n\tif len(cfg.BinEdges) < 2 {\n\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"driftmonitor: at least 2 bin edges required\")\n\t}\n\tif cfg.WindowSize <= 0 || cfg.ReferenceWindowSize <= 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"driftmonitor: window sizes must be > 0\")\n\t}\n\n\tvar writer *kafka.Writer\n\tif len(cfg.KafkaBrokers) > 0 && cfg.KafkaTopic != \"\" {\n\t\twriter = &kafka.Writer{\n\t\t\tAddr:     kafka.TCP(cfg.KafkaBrokers...),\n\t\t\tTopic:    cfg.KafkaTopic,\n\t\t\tBalancer: &kafka.CRC32Balancer{},\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn &DriftMonitor{\n\t\tcfg:       cfg,\n\t\treference: newHistogram(cfg.BinEdges, cfg.ReferenceWindowSize),\n\t\tcurrent:   newHistogram(cfg.BinEdges, cfg.WindowSize),\n\t\tfeedCh:    make(chan FeedSample, 16_384),\n\t\tobservers: make(map[Observer]struct{}),\n\t\tkafkaWriter: writer,\n\t}, nil\n}\n\n// Start launches background goroutines to consume FeedSamples and compute drift.\n// The caller can cancel the ctx to gracefully stop.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) Start(ctx context.Context) {\n\tctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(ctx)\n\tm.cancelFunc = cancel\n\n\tgo m.ingestLoop(ctx)\n\tgo m.divergenceLoop(ctx)\n}\n\n// Close stops all goroutines and flushes resources.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) Close() error {\n\tif m.cancelFunc != nil {\n\t\tm.cancelFunc()\n\t}\n\tif m.kafkaWriter != nil {\n\t\treturn m.kafkaWriter.Close()\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// Feed provides non-blocking ingestion API.  When the channel buffer is full,\n// the oldest sample is dropped to preserve real-time constraints.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) Feed(s FeedSample) {\n\tselect {\n\tcase m.feedCh <- s:\n\tdefault:\n\t\t// Channel full; drop (back-pressure mechanism)\n\t}\n}\n\n// Register attaches an observer.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) Register(o Observer) {\n\tm.observersMu.Lock()\n\tdefer m.observersMu.Unlock()\n\tm.observers[o] = struct{}{}\n}\n\n// Unregister detaches an observer.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) Unregister(o Observer) {\n\tm.observersMu.Lock()\n\tdefer m.observersMu.Unlock()\n\tdelete(m.observers, o)\n}\n\n// ----------------------------- Core Logic -----------------------------------\n\n// ingestLoop continuously reads incoming samples and updates histograms.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) ingestLoop(ctx context.Context) {\n\tfor {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\tcase s := <-m.feedCh:\n\t\t\tif !m.reference.IsFull() {\n\t\t\t\tm.reference.Insert(s.Value)\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tm.current.Insert(s.Value)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// divergenceLoop ticks at cfg.PollInterval, computes PSI and KL divergence,\n// and emits events when drift is detected.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) divergenceLoop(ctx context.Context) {\n\tticker := time.NewTicker(m.cfg.PollInterval)\n\tdefer ticker.Stop()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\tcase <-ticker.C:\n\t\t\tif !m.reference.IsFull() || !m.current.IsFull() {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tpsi := populationStabilityIndex(m.reference, m.current)\n\t\t\tkl := klDivergence(m.reference, m.current)\n\n\t\t\tswitch {\n\t\t\tcase psi >= m.cfg.MinPSI:\n\t\t\t\tm.emitDrift(ctx, \"psi\", psi, 0)\n\t\t\tcase kl >= m.cfg.MinKL:\n\t\t\t\tm.emitDrift(ctx, \"kl\", kl, 0)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// emitDrift builds a DriftEvent and notifies all downstream observers / Kafka.\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) emitDrift(ctx context.Context, metric string, value, p float64) {\n\te := DriftEvent{\n\t\tEventID:        uuid.NewString(),\n\t\tModelName:      m.cfg.ModelName,\n\t\tFeatureName:    \"global\", // TODO: extend per feature\n\t\tMetric:         metric,\n\t\tValue:          value,\n\t\tPValue:         p,\n\t\tWindowSize:     m.cfg.WindowSize,\n\t\tReferenceSince: m.reference.startTime,\n\t\tTimestamp:      time.Now().UTC(),\n\t}\n\n\t// Fan-out to registered observers\n\tm.observersMu.RLock()\n\tfor o := range m.observers {\n\t\tgo o.OnDrift(ctx, e) // invoke asynchronously\n\t}\n\tm.observersMu.RUnlock()\n\n\t// Optionally publish to Kafka\n\tif m.kafkaWriter != nil {\n\t\tpayload, _ := json.Marshal(e) // safe: never fails for struct\n\t\t_ = m.kafkaWriter.WriteMessages(ctx, kafka.Message{\n\t\t\tKey:   []byte(e.EventID),\n\t\t\tValue: payload,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\n// ------------------------ Histogram / Metrics ------------------------------\n\ntype histogram struct {\n\tbins       []int\n\tedges      []float64\n\tmaxSamples int\n\tsamples    int\n\tstartTime  time.Time\n\tmu         sync.Mutex\n}\n\nfunc newHistogram(edges []float64, max int) *histogram {\n\treturn &histogram{\n\t\tbins:       make([]int, len(edges)+1), // extra bin for right-most edge\n\t\tedges:      edges,\n\t\tmaxSamples: max,\n\t\tstartTime:  time.Now(),\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (h *histogram) Insert(v float64) {\n\th.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer h.mu.Unlock()\n\n\t// drop old sample if full (simple circular buffer semantics ‑ degrade oldest bin proportionally)\n\tif h.samples >= h.maxSamples {\n\t\t// naive: scale down counts by ratio to make room for 1 sample\n\t\tfor i := range h.bins {\n\t\t\tif h.bins[i] > 0 {\n\t\t\t\th.bins[i] = int(math.Round(float64(h.bins[i]) * float64(h.maxSamples-1) / float64(h.maxSamples)))\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\th.samples = h.maxSamples - 1\n\t}\n\n\tidx := h.index(v)\n\th.bins[idx]++\n\th.samples++\n}\n\nfunc (h *histogram) IsFull() bool {\n\th.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer h.mu.Unlock()\n\treturn h.samples >= h.maxSamples\n}\n\nfunc (h *histogram) index(v float64) int {\n\tfor i, edge := range h.edges {\n\t\tif v <= edge {\n\t\t\treturn i\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn len(h.edges) // right-most bin\n}\n\n// toProbs copies and converts counts to probability distribution.\nfunc (h *histogram) toProbs() []float64 {\n\th.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer h.mu.Unlock()\n\n\ttotal := float64(h.samples)\n\tp := make([]float64, len(h.bins))\n\tif total == 0 {\n\t\treturn p\n\t}\n\tfor i, c := range h.bins {\n\t\tp[i] = float64(c) / total\n\t}\n\treturn p\n}\n\n// populationStabilityIndex calculates PSI between reference and production\n// distributions.  A typical rule: PSI > 0.25 signals significant drift.\nfunc populationStabilityIndex(ref, cur *histogram) float64 {\n\tr := ref.toProbs()\n\tp := cur.toProbs()\n\n\tconst epsilon = 1e-12\n\tpsi := 0.0\n\tfor i := range r {\n\t\texpected := r[i] + epsilon\n\t\tactual := p[i] + epsilon\n\t\tpsi += (actual - expected) * math.Log(actual/expected)\n\t}\n\treturn psi\n}\n\n// klDivergence calculates Kullback-Leibler divergence.\nfunc klDivergence(ref, cur *histogram) float64 {\n\tr := ref.toProbs()\n\tp := cur.toProbs()\n\n\tconst epsilon = 1e-12\n\tkl := 0.0\n\tfor i := range r {\n\t\tq := r[i] + epsilon\n\t\td := p[i] + epsilon\n\t\tkl += d * math.Log(d/q)\n\t}\n\treturn kl\n}\n```","numLines":352,"startLine":1,"totalLines":352}}}

Down-stream services (sentiment, toxicity, etc.)\n// decorate it with additional attributes; we only need a subset here.\ntype SocialEvent struct {\n\tID           string            `json:\"id\"`\n\tUserID       string            `json:\"user_id\"`\n\tChannelID    string            `json:\"channel_id\"`\n\tPayload      json.RawMessage   `json:\"payload\"`       // raw content\n\tExtracted    map[string]any    `json:\"extracted\"`     // enriched features\n\tCreatedAtUTC time.Time         `json:\"created_at_utc\"`// event time\n\tMetadata     map[string]string `json:\"metadata\"`      // transport metadata\n}\n```\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_54.go\n// Lines: 358-400\n\n// Run wires up subscriptions and blocks until ctx is cancelled.\n// It spawns two goroutines:\n//   1. eventLoop:   consumes incoming SocialEvents\n//   2. windowLoop:  periodically evaluates completed windows\nfunc (s *CommunityScorer) Run(ctx context.Context, topicIn, topicOut string) error {\n\tevtCh, err := s.sub.Subscribe(ctx, topicIn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Using errgroup ensures both goroutines finish cleanly.\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\twg.Add(2)\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\ts.eventLoop(ctx, evtCh)\n\t}()\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\ts.windowLoop(ctx, topicOut)\n\t}()\n\t// ...\n}\n```\n\n### Module 69 - NLP Processing Layer (Sentiment Aggregator)\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_69.go\n// Lines: 78-84\n\n// SentimentAnalyzer scores arbitrary text in a given language.\ntype SentimentAnalyzer interface {\n\t// Analyze should return a polarity in the range [-1, 1].\n\tAnalyze(text, lang string) (float64, error)\n\t// Name is used for metrics / debugging.\n\tName() string\n}\n```\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_69.go\n// Lines: 357-375\n\n// runProcessingLoop is event-driven: it consumes SocialEvents, scores them,\n// and updates rolling stats.\nfunc (a *SlidingWindowAggregator) runProcessingLoop() {\n\tdefer a.wg.Done()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase evt := <-a.input:\n\t\t\tscore, err := a.analyzer.Analyze(evt.Payload, evt.Language)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t// log and skip – for brevity we print to stderr.\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Printf(\"sentiment analyze error: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\ta.ingestScore(evt, score)\n\n\t\tcase <-a.ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Module 25 - Storage/Drift Detection Layer\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_25.go\n// Lines: 31-44\n\n// FeatureStore provides access to persistent reference distributions that were\n// calculated during model training/validation.  This abstraction hides the\n// implementation details (SQL, S3, in-memory, …).\ntype FeatureStore interface {\n\tGetReferenceDistribution(model, feature string) (map[string]float64, error)\n}\n```\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_25.go\n// Lines: 144-189\n\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) loop() {\n\tdefer m.wg.Done()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tmsg, err := m.reader.ReadMessage(m.ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {\n\t\t\t\treturn // normal shutdown\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tlogrus.WithError(err).Warn(\"driftmonitor: read error\")\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar metric DriftMetric\n\t\tif err = json.Unmarshal(msg.Value, &metric); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlogrus.WithError(err).Warn(\"driftmonitor: invalid json payload\")\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\t// ... drift detection and handler invocation\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Module 31 - Feature Store Write Operations\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_31.go\n// Lines: 347-364\n\n// persistFeatureVector upserts the vector in the Feature Store via gRPC.\nfunc (s *FeatureExtractorService) persistFeatureVector(ctx context.Context, fv *FeatureVector) error {\n\treq := &pb.PutFeatureVectorRequest{\n\t\tVector: &pb.FeatureVector{\n\t\t\tEventId:      fv.EventID,\n\t\t\tUserId:       fv.UserID,\n\t\t\tChannelId:    fv.ChannelID,\n\t\t\tSentiment:    fv.Sentiment,\n\t\t\tEmojiDensity: fv.EmojiDensity,\n\t\t\tWordCount:    int32(fv.WordCount),\n\t\t\tLanguage:     fv.Language,\n\t\t\tGeneratedAt:  fv.GeneratedAt.UnixNano(),\n\t\t\tMetadata:     fv.AdditionalMeta,\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\t_, err := s.fsClient.PutFeatureVector(ctx, req)\n\treturn err\n}\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Data Flow Mapping\n\nThe data flow from signal reception to feature store storage follows this path:\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐\n│   module_54     │     │   module_69     │     │   module_25     │\n│   (INGEST)      │────►│   (PROCESS)     │────►│   (STORE)       │\n│                 │     │                 │     │                 │\n│ - SocialEvent   │     │ - Sentiment     │     │ - FeatureStore  │\n│ - EventBus      │     │   Analyzer      │     │ - DriftMonitor  │\n│ - Community     │     │ - Sliding       │     │ - Kafka Writer  │\n│   Scorer        │     │   WindowAgg     │     │ - HTTP Client   │\n└─────────────────┘     └─────────────────┘     └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Detailed Flow:**\n1. **Ingestion (`module_54`)**: Receives `SocialEvent` via event bus subscription, validates and normalizes the payload\n2. **Processing (`module_69`)**: Performs NLP-based feature extraction using `SentimentAnalyzer` strategy, computes sentiment scores via VADER or similar models\n3. **Storage (`module_25`)**: Writes extracted features to the feature store via gRPC, monitors for drift\n\n### 2. Architectural Bottleneck Identification\n\n**PRIMARY BOTTLENECK: Synchronous Execution of CPU-bound Processing and I/O within a Single Request Path**\n\nThe current architecture exhibits several critical scalability issues:\n\n#### a) Synchronous Processing Chain\nLooking at `module_69.go:357-375`, the `runProcessingLoop()` function processes events synchronously:\n```go\nfor {\n    select {\n    case evt := <-a.input:\n        score, err := a.analyzer.Analyze(evt.Payload, evt.Language)  // CPU-bound NLP\n        // ...\n        a.ingestScore(evt, score)  // I/O operation\n```\n\nEach event must complete sentiment analysis (CPU-bound) before the next event can be processed from the channel.\n\n#### b) Blocking I/O in Critical Path\nIn `module_31.go:291-293`, the feature vector persistence is performed synchronously within the processing loop:\n```go\nif err := s.persistFeatureVector(ctx, fv); err != nil {\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\nThis gRPC call blocks the worker until the feature store acknowledges the write.\n\n#### c) Limited Worker Pool Scaling\nWhile the codebase uses worker pools (e.g., `module_31.go:206-213`), the workers still perform CPU-bound NLP and blocking I/O sequentially:\n```go\nfor i := 0; i < s.cfg.Concurrency; i++ {\n    s.wg.Add(1)\n    go s.workerLoop(i)\n}\n```\n\nUnder 10x load, this pattern fails because:\n- CPU-bound NLP operations become the bottleneck\n- Each worker blocks waiting for feature store I/O\n- Request queues fill up, causing timeouts\n- Back-pressure propagates to ingestion, causing data loss\n\n#### d) Tight Coupling Without Buffering\nThe modules are tightly coupled through direct function calls or synchronous channel operations. There's no durable message queue between stages, meaning:\n- A slow downstream component (e.g., feature store) blocks upstream processing\n- No retry mechanism for failed operations\n- No ability to scale processing independently of ingestion\n\n### 3. Refactoring Strategy\n\n**Proposed Architecture: Decoupled Asynchronous Pipeline with Message Queue**\n\n```\n┌────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐    ┌────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐    ┌────────────┐\n│  Ingestion │    │   Message    │    │  Worker    │    │   Message    │    │  Storage   │\n│  Gateway   │───►│   Queue 1    │───►│  Pool      │───►│   Queue 2    │───►│  Workers   │\n│ (module_54)│    │  (Kafka/     │    │ (NLP/ML)   │    │  (Kafka/     │    │ (module_25)│\n│            │    │   NATS)      │    │ (module_69)│    │   NATS)      │    │            │\n│ HTTP 202   │    │              │    │            │    │              │    │ Feature    │\n│ Accepted   │    │ Durable      │    │ Auto-scale │    │ Durable      │    │ Store      │\n└────────────┘    └──────────────┘    └────────────┘    └──────────────┘    └────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Phase 1: Decouple Ingestion from Processing\n\n**Change to `module_54` (Ingestion):**\n- Convert HTTP handlers to return `202 Accepted` immediately after validating and publishing to Kafka/NATS\n- Remove synchronous calls to downstream processing\n- Add a `SocialEventProducer` interface that writes to the message queue\n\n```go\n// Proposed change to ingestion handler\nfunc (h *IngestHandler) HandleSignal(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n    var event SocialEvent\n    if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&event); err != nil {\n        http.Error(w, \"Invalid payload\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n        return\n    }\n\n    // Publish to message queue (non-blocking)\n    if err := h.producer.Publish(ctx, \"social-events\", event); err != nil {\n        http.Error(w, \"Queue unavailable\", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)\n        return\n    }\n\n    w.WriteHeader(http.StatusAccepted)  // Return 202 immediately\n    json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{\"event_id\": event.ID})\n}\n```\n\n#### Phase 2: Implement Asynchronous Processing Workers\n\n**Change to `module_69` (NLP Processing):**\n- Create a pool of consumer workers that pull from Kafka/NATS\n- Process events in parallel without blocking on I/O\n- Publish processed features to a second queue\n\n```go\n// Proposed worker pool for NLP processing\ntype ProcessingWorkerPool struct {\n    consumer    MessageConsumer\n    producer    MessageProducer\n    analyzer    SentimentAnalyzer\n    workerCount int\n}\n\nfunc (p *ProcessingWorkerPool) Start(ctx context.Context) {\n    for i := 0; i < p.workerCount; i++ {\n        go p.worker(ctx, i)\n    }\n}\n\nfunc (p *ProcessingWorkerPool) worker(ctx context.Context, id int) {\n    for {\n        msg, err := p.consumer.FetchMessage(ctx)\n        if err != nil {\n            continue\n        }\n\n        // CPU-bound NLP processing\n        features := p.extractFeatures(msg)\n\n        // Non-blocking publish to storage queue\n        p.producer.Publish(ctx, \"feature-vectors\", features)\n        p.consumer.CommitMessage(msg)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Phase 3: Asynchronous Feature Store Writers\n\n**Change to `module_25` (Storage):**\n- Create dedicated consumer group for feature store writes\n- Implement batch writes to reduce I/O overhead\n- Add retry logic with exponential backoff\n\n```go\n// Proposed batch writer for feature store\ntype FeatureStoreWriter struct {\n    consumer    MessageConsumer\n    fsClient    FeatureStoreClient\n    batchSize   int\n    flushPeriod time.Duration\n}\n\nfunc (w *FeatureStoreWriter) worker(ctx context.Context) {\n    batch := make([]*FeatureVector, 0, w.batchSize)\n    ticker := time.NewTicker(w.flushPeriod)\n\n    for {\n        select {\n        case msg := <-w.consumer.Messages():\n            batch = append(batch, parseFeatureVector(msg))\n            if len(batch) >= w.batchSize {\n                w.flushBatch(ctx, batch)\n                batch = batch[:0]\n            }\n        case <-ticker.C:\n            if len(batch) > 0 {\n                w.flushBatch(ctx, batch)\n                batch = batch[:0]\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Justification and Benefits\n\n#### Throughput Improvement\n\n**Current State:**\n- Single-threaded processing per worker\n- 100 events/sec per worker (limited by NLP latency)\n- With 10 workers: ~1,000 events/sec\n\n**Proposed State:**\n- Decoupled stages can scale independently\n- Ingestion: ~100,000 events/sec (network I/O bound)\n- Processing: Horizontally scalable worker pods\n- Storage: Batch writes reduce per-event overhead\n\n#### Latency Characteristics\n\n**Current State:**\n- End-to-end latency: 200-500ms (NLP + feature store write)\n- P99 latency spikes during feature store slowdowns\n\n**Proposed State:**\n- Ingestion latency: ~5ms (queue publish)\n- Processing latency: Decoupled, not customer-facing\n- Eventual consistency acceptable for analytics\n\n#### System Resilience\n\n**Current State:**\n- Feature store outage causes ingestion failures\n- No retry mechanism for transient errors\n- Lost events during processing crashes\n\n**Proposed State:**\n- Feature store outage: Events buffered in queue\n- Kafka/NATS provides at-least-once delivery\n- Consumer offset tracking enables crash recovery\n\n#### Recommended Technologies\n\n1. **Message Queue**: Kafka (already in `config.go`) or NATS JetStream\n2. **Worker Pool**: Use Go's `errgroup` with configurable concurrency\n3. **Batch Processing**: Implement micro-batching for feature store writes\n4. **Circuit Breaker**: Add circuit breaker for feature store calls\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The architectural bottleneck is the **synchronous execution of CPU-bound NLP processing and blocking I/O (feature store writes) within a single request/response cycle**. This tight coupling prevents horizontal scaling and causes cascading failures under load.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - `module_54` (ingestion): Receives SocialEvents via event bus\n  - `module_69` (processing): Performs sentiment analysis and feature extraction\n  - `module_25` (storage): Writes to feature store and monitors drift\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The current architecture uses a **synchronous request/response model** with tightly coupled components. The proposed solution implements an **asynchronous, queue-based pipeline architecture** with decoupled stages.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Decouple ingestion from processing using Kafka/NATS message queue\n  2. Return HTTP 202 Accepted immediately after queue publish\n  3. Implement auto-scaling worker pools for NLP processing\n  4. Add batch writes and retry logic for feature store operations\n  5. Use circuit breaker pattern for external service calls\n\nThis refactoring enables **10x+ throughput scaling**, improves **system resilience**, and allows **independent scaling** of each pipeline stage while leveraging the existing Kafka infrastructure configured in `config.go`.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":354,"cache_read_input_tokens":145370,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":354,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dd6facce-874b-4cea-b19e-1fa9102e7f99","uuid":"da61fdc4-d707-4398-8fb2-60db3707df11"}

Down-stream services (sentiment, toxicity, etc.)\n// decorate it with additional attributes; we only need a subset here.\ntype SocialEvent struct {\n\tID           string            `json:\"id\"`\n\tUserID       string            `json:\"user_id\"`\n\tChannelID    string            `json:\"channel_id\"`\n\tPayload      json.RawMessage   `json:\"payload\"`       // raw content\n\tExtracted    map[string]any    `json:\"extracted\"`     // enriched features\n\tCreatedAtUTC time.Time         `json:\"created_at_utc\"`// event time\n\tMetadata     map[string]string `json:\"metadata\"`      // transport metadata\n}\n```\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_54.go\n// Lines: 358-400\n\n// Run wires up subscriptions and blocks until ctx is cancelled.\n// It spawns two goroutines:\n//   1. eventLoop:   consumes incoming SocialEvents\n//   2. windowLoop:  periodically evaluates completed windows\nfunc (s *CommunityScorer) Run(ctx context.Context, topicIn, topicOut string) error {\n\tevtCh, err := s.sub.Subscribe(ctx, topicIn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Using errgroup ensures both goroutines finish cleanly.\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\twg.Add(2)\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\ts.eventLoop(ctx, evtCh)\n\t}()\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\ts.windowLoop(ctx, topicOut)\n\t}()\n\t// ...\n}\n```\n\n### Module 69 - NLP Processing Layer (Sentiment Aggregator)\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_69.go\n// Lines: 78-84\n\n// SentimentAnalyzer scores arbitrary text in a given language.\ntype SentimentAnalyzer interface {\n\t// Analyze should return a polarity in the range [-1, 1].\n\tAnalyze(text, lang string) (float64, error)\n\t// Name is used for metrics / debugging.\n\tName() string\n}\n```\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_69.go\n// Lines: 357-375\n\n// runProcessingLoop is event-driven: it consumes SocialEvents, scores them,\n// and updates rolling stats.\nfunc (a *SlidingWindowAggregator) runProcessingLoop() {\n\tdefer a.wg.Done()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase evt := <-a.input:\n\t\t\tscore, err := a.analyzer.Analyze(evt.Payload, evt.Language)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t// log and skip – for brevity we print to stderr.\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Printf(\"sentiment analyze error: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\ta.ingestScore(evt, score)\n\n\t\tcase <-a.ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Module 25 - Storage/Drift Detection Layer\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_25.go\n// Lines: 31-44\n\n// FeatureStore provides access to persistent reference distributions that were\n// calculated during model training/validation.  This abstraction hides the\n// implementation details (SQL, S3, in-memory, …).\ntype FeatureStore interface {\n\tGetReferenceDistribution(model, feature string) (map[string]float64, error)\n}\n```\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_25.go\n// Lines: 144-189\n\nfunc (m *DriftMonitor) loop() {\n\tdefer m.wg.Done()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tmsg, err := m.reader.ReadMessage(m.ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {\n\t\t\t\treturn // normal shutdown\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tlogrus.WithError(err).Warn(\"driftmonitor: read error\")\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar metric DriftMetric\n\t\tif err = json.Unmarshal(msg.Value, &metric); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlogrus.WithError(err).Warn(\"driftmonitor: invalid json payload\")\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\t// ... drift detection and handler invocation\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Module 31 - Feature Store Write Operations\n\n```go\n// File: /app/project/src/module_31.go\n// Lines: 347-364\n\n// persistFeatureVector upserts the vector in the Feature Store via gRPC.\nfunc (s *FeatureExtractorService) persistFeatureVector(ctx context.Context, fv *FeatureVector) error {\n\treq := &pb.PutFeatureVectorRequest{\n\t\tVector: &pb.FeatureVector{\n\t\t\tEventId:      fv.EventID,\n\t\t\tUserId:       fv.UserID,\n\t\t\tChannelId:    fv.ChannelID,\n\t\t\tSentiment:    fv.Sentiment,\n\t\t\tEmojiDensity: fv.EmojiDensity,\n\t\t\tWordCount:    int32(fv.WordCount),\n\t\t\tLanguage:     fv.Language,\n\t\t\tGeneratedAt:  fv.GeneratedAt.UnixNano(),\n\t\t\tMetadata:     fv.AdditionalMeta,\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\t_, err := s.fsClient.PutFeatureVector(ctx, req)\n\treturn err\n}\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Data Flow Mapping\n\nThe data flow from signal reception to feature store storage follows this path:\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐\n│   module_54     │     │   module_69     │     │   module_25     │\n│   (INGEST)      │────►│   (PROCESS)     │────►│   (STORE)       │\n│                 │     │                 │     │                 │\n│ - SocialEvent   │     │ - Sentiment     │     │ - FeatureStore  │\n│ - EventBus      │     │   Analyzer      │     │ - DriftMonitor  │\n│ - Community     │     │ - Sliding       │     │ - Kafka Writer  │\n│   Scorer        │     │   WindowAgg     │     │ - HTTP Client   │\n└─────────────────┘     └─────────────────┘     └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Detailed Flow:**\n1. **Ingestion (`module_54`)**: Receives `SocialEvent` via event bus subscription, validates and normalizes the payload\n2. **Processing (`module_69`)**: Performs NLP-based feature extraction using `SentimentAnalyzer` strategy, computes sentiment scores via VADER or similar models\n3. **Storage (`module_25`)**: Writes extracted features to the feature store via gRPC, monitors for drift\n\n### 2. Architectural Bottleneck Identification\n\n**PRIMARY BOTTLENECK: Synchronous Execution of CPU-bound Processing and I/O within a Single Request Path**\n\nThe current architecture exhibits several critical scalability issues:\n\n#### a) Synchronous Processing Chain\nLooking at `module_69.go:357-375`, the `runProcessingLoop()` function processes events synchronously:\n```go\nfor {\n    select {\n    case evt := <-a.input:\n        score, err := a.analyzer.Analyze(evt.Payload, evt.Language)  // CPU-bound NLP\n        // ...\n        a.ingestScore(evt, score)  // I/O operation\n```\n\nEach event must complete sentiment analysis (CPU-bound) before the next event can be processed from the channel.\n\n#### b) Blocking I/O in Critical Path\nIn `module_31.go:291-293`, the feature vector persistence is performed synchronously within the processing loop:\n```go\nif err := s.persistFeatureVector(ctx, fv); err != nil {\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\nThis gRPC call blocks the worker until the feature store acknowledges the write.\n\n#### c) Limited Worker Pool Scaling\nWhile the codebase uses worker pools (e.g., `module_31.go:206-213`), the workers still perform CPU-bound NLP and blocking I/O sequentially:\n```go\nfor i := 0; i < s.cfg.Concurrency; i++ {\n    s.wg.Add(1)\n    go s.workerLoop(i)\n}\n```\n\nUnder 10x load, this pattern fails because:\n- CPU-bound NLP operations become the bottleneck\n- Each worker blocks waiting for feature store I/O\n- Request queues fill up, causing timeouts\n- Back-pressure propagates to ingestion, causing data loss\n\n#### d) Tight Coupling Without Buffering\nThe modules are tightly coupled through direct function calls or synchronous channel operations. There's no durable message queue between stages, meaning:\n- A slow downstream component (e.g., feature store) blocks upstream processing\n- No retry mechanism for failed operations\n- No ability to scale processing independently of ingestion\n\n### 3. Refactoring Strategy\n\n**Proposed Architecture: Decoupled Asynchronous Pipeline with Message Queue**\n\n```\n┌────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐    ┌────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐    ┌────────────┐\n│  Ingestion │    │   Message    │    │  Worker    │    │   Message    │    │  Storage   │\n│  Gateway   │───►│   Queue 1    │───►│  Pool      │───►│   Queue 2    │───►│  Workers   │\n│ (module_54)│    │  (Kafka/     │    │ (NLP/ML)   │    │  (Kafka/     │    │ (module_25)│\n│            │    │   NATS)      │    │ (module_69)│    │   NATS)      │    │            │\n│ HTTP 202   │    │              │    │            │    │              │    │ Feature    │\n│ Accepted   │    │ Durable      │    │ Auto-scale │    │ Durable      │    │ Store      │\n└────────────┘    └──────────────┘    └────────────┘    └──────────────┘    └────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Phase 1: Decouple Ingestion from Processing\n\n**Change to `module_54` (Ingestion):**\n- Convert HTTP handlers to return `202 Accepted` immediately after validating and publishing to Kafka/NATS\n- Remove synchronous calls to downstream processing\n- Add a `SocialEventProducer` interface that writes to the message queue\n\n```go\n// Proposed change to ingestion handler\nfunc (h *IngestHandler) HandleSignal(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n    var event SocialEvent\n    if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&event); err != nil {\n        http.Error(w, \"Invalid payload\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n        return\n    }\n\n    // Publish to message queue (non-blocking)\n    if err := h.producer.Publish(ctx, \"social-events\", event); err != nil {\n        http.Error(w, \"Queue unavailable\", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)\n        return\n    }\n\n    w.WriteHeader(http.StatusAccepted)  // Return 202 immediately\n    json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{\"event_id\": event.ID})\n}\n```\n\n#### Phase 2: Implement Asynchronous Processing Workers\n\n**Change to `module_69` (NLP Processing):**\n- Create a pool of consumer workers that pull from Kafka/NATS\n- Process events in parallel without blocking on I/O\n- Publish processed features to a second queue\n\n```go\n// Proposed worker pool for NLP processing\ntype ProcessingWorkerPool struct {\n    consumer    MessageConsumer\n    producer    MessageProducer\n    analyzer    SentimentAnalyzer\n    workerCount int\n}\n\nfunc (p *ProcessingWorkerPool) Start(ctx context.Context) {\n    for i := 0; i < p.workerCount; i++ {\n        go p.worker(ctx, i)\n    }\n}\n\nfunc (p *ProcessingWorkerPool) worker(ctx context.Context, id int) {\n    for {\n        msg, err := p.consumer.FetchMessage(ctx)\n        if err != nil {\n            continue\n        }\n\n        // CPU-bound NLP processing\n        features := p.extractFeatures(msg)\n\n        // Non-blocking publish to storage queue\n        p.producer.Publish(ctx, \"feature-vectors\", features)\n        p.consumer.CommitMessage(msg)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Phase 3: Asynchronous Feature Store Writers\n\n**Change to `module_25` (Storage):**\n- Create dedicated consumer group for feature store writes\n- Implement batch writes to reduce I/O overhead\n- Add retry logic with exponential backoff\n\n```go\n// Proposed batch writer for feature store\ntype FeatureStoreWriter struct {\n    consumer    MessageConsumer\n    fsClient    FeatureStoreClient\n    batchSize   int\n    flushPeriod time.Duration\n}\n\nfunc (w *FeatureStoreWriter) worker(ctx context.Context) {\n    batch := make([]*FeatureVector, 0, w.batchSize)\n    ticker := time.NewTicker(w.flushPeriod)\n\n    for {\n        select {\n        case msg := <-w.consumer.Messages():\n            batch = append(batch, parseFeatureVector(msg))\n            if len(batch) >= w.batchSize {\n                w.flushBatch(ctx, batch)\n                batch = batch[:0]\n            }\n        case <-ticker.C:\n            if len(batch) > 0 {\n                w.flushBatch(ctx, batch)\n                batch = batch[:0]\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Justification and Benefits\n\n#### Throughput Improvement\n\n**Current State:**\n- Single-threaded processing per worker\n- 100 events/sec per worker (limited by NLP latency)\n- With 10 workers: ~1,000 events/sec\n\n**Proposed State:**\n- Decoupled stages can scale independently\n- Ingestion: ~100,000 events/sec (network I/O bound)\n- Processing: Horizontally scalable worker pods\n- Storage: Batch writes reduce per-event overhead\n\n#### Latency Characteristics\n\n**Current State:**\n- End-to-end latency: 200-500ms (NLP + feature store write)\n- P99 latency spikes during feature store slowdowns\n\n**Proposed State:**\n- Ingestion latency: ~5ms (queue publish)\n- Processing latency: Decoupled, not customer-facing\n- Eventual consistency acceptable for analytics\n\n#### System Resilience\n\n**Current State:**\n- Feature store outage causes ingestion failures\n- No retry mechanism for transient errors\n- Lost events during processing crashes\n\n**Proposed State:**\n- Feature store outage: Events buffered in queue\n- Kafka/NATS provides at-least-once delivery\n- Consumer offset tracking enables crash recovery\n\n#### Recommended Technologies\n\n1. **Message Queue**: Kafka (already in `config.go`) or NATS JetStream\n2. **Worker Pool**: Use Go's `errgroup` with configurable concurrency\n3. **Batch Processing**: Implement micro-batching for feature store writes\n4. **Circuit Breaker**: Add circuit breaker for feature store calls\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The architectural bottleneck is the **synchronous execution of CPU-bound NLP processing and blocking I/O (feature store writes) within a single request/response cycle**. This tight coupling prevents horizontal scaling and causes cascading failures under load.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - `module_54` (ingestion): Receives SocialEvents via event bus\n  - `module_69` (processing): Performs sentiment analysis and feature extraction\n  - `module_25` (storage): Writes to feature store and monitors drift\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The current architecture uses a **synchronous request/response model** with tightly coupled components. The proposed solution implements an **asynchronous, queue-based pipeline architecture** with decoupled stages.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Decouple ingestion from processing using Kafka/NATS message queue\n  2. Return HTTP 202 Accepted immediately after queue publish\n  3. Implement auto-scaling worker pools for NLP processing\n  4. Add batch writes and retry logic for feature store operations\n  5. Use circuit breaker pattern for external service calls\n\nThis refactoring enables **10x+ throughput scaling**, improves **system resilience**, and allows **independent scaling** of each pipeline stage while leveraging the existing Kafka infrastructure configured in `config.go`.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
